Unfortunately, this is moving Denmark in a direction where the state plays a greater role and where large capital societies, pension schemes and others will play an even greater role instead of these family businesses. This is a development that we absolutely do not like in New Citizens, and that is why we can support a proposal to reduce generation taxation. But we also believe that we should go further. We believe that it must be completely abolished, because when we have problems with it being uprooted, it is because it has not been abolished, and then it is because the last government unfortunately did not have the courage to abolish it, because then we would have to introduce a whole new tax. I hope that when the majority changes, and I hope that soon it does, it can be a common bourgeois project to have this tax removed and removed completely, so that one cannot talk about reclaiming it so polemically and rhetorically saying that one is reclaiming the tax, or anything else. For no, if you want to introduce an inheritance tax or a generation tax, you have to introduce a new tax on family businesses, and you have to stand by that.

ParlaMint-DK_20210415215849	Thank you. Thank you for the debate, which was perhaps not so surprising. In Denmark we have 60 000 family-owned companies and, as the Conservative rapporteur said, they contribute 70%. of Danish exports. In the region I come from, Region Sjælland, 90%. of all companies owned and family owned. That is a very, very large number of jobs. 850,000 jobs in Denmark are in owner-managed and family-owned companies. This means that you have to take more money out of the company when it is that you have to change the generation; when a new generation is going to. That is not what we are talking about; what we are talking about is that there is a new generation to take over the company because the old generation is getting too old. That is why we need a new generation; otherwise the company cannot survive. They have to pay a tax for that; there is a penalty for that. And it is actually very expensive, because not only do you have to pay the generational change tax, no, because when you take the money out of the company, you also have to pay the dividend tax, tax of a lot of different things. For some companies it may amount to 80%. of their equity, they have to pay in tax because they have to change generation. Not all companies can stand up to it. Some people simply have to sell themselves to others, and we know that not all companies are as faithful in maintaining jobs in Denmark as the family owners are. For the family owners are in Denmark because they belong in Denmark. Their family is in Denmark. They are engaged in the local area to a degree that you don't see many other types of companies being. If you sell it to a foreign fund, it is easier to move jobs abroad. And it is very important for the local area if jobs just disappear. It is important that we have the family-owned companies that invest in local areas and that invest in jobs locally. We can see from studies that some of the companies are investing a lot in developing companies too. It is of great importance that they be drained of capital in this way, and in the end it will cost jobs and growth for Denmark. This government chose to raise the tax from 6%. 15%. It's a massive increase. The government says they did it because we lowered it from 15%. to 6%, and as we have already heard in the debate, this is due to the fact that in 2015 the then Social Democratic tax minister abolished the valuation that was of the generational change of family-owned companies, so it is now much harder to assess how much they have to pay. This also meant that the assessment increased significantly, and the revenue that was paid in extra, we then chose to spend on lowering the rate instead. So we got the same revenue as they did before the Socialists abolished the circular; it was just a cleaner way of implementing this. But it must obviously be expensive to have family-owned companies in Denmark. One might almost suspect that there is a vendetta against family-owned companies in Denmark • despite the growth and the many jobs that we get from the family-owned companies. There is no doubt that this is going to mean that jobs are moving out of the country. And that's why we're really sorry that this is supposed to be something that separates in half. We would like this to be something that we could seek a majority in several places, because we think it ought to be an agenda that several of us shared in Denmark: to secure these jobs in Denmark. Thanks for the treatment.
